#3065. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#3060. Federal Reserve Board requests public comment on two proposals related to establishing a regulatory framework for Board-supervised payment stablecoin issuers under the GENIUS Act

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:30 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday requested public comment on two proposals related to establishing a regulatory framework for Board-supervised payment stablecoin issuers under the GENIUS Act.

The first proposal would require that Board-supervised payment stablecoin issuers fully back their stablecoins with certain permissible reserve assets, such as short-term Treasury bills and certain other high-quality, liquid assets. The proposal would also establish standardized capital requirements to address certain credit and operational risks of payment stablecoin activities, as well as risk management standards, in accordance with the law. Separately, the proposal would introduce rules for Board-supervised firms that safekeep the assets backing payment stablecoins. It would also clarify the permissibility of stablecoin and related activities for Board-supervised banks.

The second proposal would establish a tailored application process for Board-supervised banks applying to issue payment stablecoins. These applicants would be required to submit a business plan and financial information, among other documents. The proposal would also create a process governing appeals, hearings, and final determinations for applications.

The comment period will close 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call (202) 452-2955.

#3058. Chargers haven't scored a touchdown in weeks. Mike McDaniel hopes a new view will help

Mike McDaniel is shifting his perspective in an attempt to revive the Chargers' dead offense.

Although he usually operates on the sideline and behind the line of scrimmage during games, the offensive coordinator said he'll call plays from the SoFi Stadium coaches' box during Sunday's matchup against the Houston Texans.

"One of the things I talked with [coach Jim Harbaugh] about … I want to try [to] call plays from the box to kind of see adjustments for myself and communicate to the position coaches appropriately what things we can focus on between series," McDaniel said. "I'm being very open-minded with all things, not trying to prove or justify anything that ultimately leads to underwhelming results."

Read more: Chargers vs. Texans: How to watch, start time, prediction and odds

Added Harbaugh: "I think it's good. ... I mean, nobody watches more tape than Mike. And that's the view; that's the vision. I think it's good, and [I'm] all for it."

McDaniel and his unit could desperately use a change.

The 0-7 Chargers, who haven't scored a touchdown since Oct. 11, enter the matinee averaging the NFL's fewest points per game (14.1) and fewest expected points added per play (-.027). They've also had a league-high 17 turnovers.

And Justin Herbert, who has an NFL-worst 10 interceptions and is 30th in QBR (32.5), is having the most dreadful season of his seven-year career.

"Seems like a good action to take based on how things have gone this year, and realistically something that I've only brought up because I'm very confident in the rest of the offensive coaching staff and how they're able to set the tone and communicate information on the sidelines," McDaniel said of his move.

Rams linebacker Wesley Bailey, left, forces Chargers quarterback Justin Herbert to fumble on Nov. 1. (Robert Gauthier / Los Angeles Times)

McDaniel last called plays from the coaches' box during his one season as the San Francisco 49ers' offensive coordinator in 2021. That team averaged 25.1 points per game as a 10-7 wild card.

He credits the shift to 49ers coach Kyle Shanahan, who called plays from the coaches' box as the Falcons' offensive coordinator when McDaniel worked alongside him in Atlanta from 2015-16 before their overlap in the Bay Area.

"It's like watching tape," McDaniel said. "You train yourself over time to see the game on the field. One of the reasons I stand behind the ball so far [on the field] is so that I can try to get a visual of all 22; because [otherwise], you're so close to the ball you can kind of have your periphery shut out. That's not the case from the box.

"In an ideal world, if all things are equal and there's no negative from not being on the field, you can see the game better from up there."

Read more: Rams' Quentin Lake texted apology to Chargers Justin Herbert for ham-handed 'deli meat' comment

McDaniel valued calling plays from the sideline because of the face-to-face communication with Herbert and other members of the Chargers' offense. But that didn't lead to consistent scoring, as the team has surpassed 17 points in a game just once this season.

Desperate times call for desperate measures — especially when your job is potentially at stake.

"My problem-solving doesn't come from insecurity," McDaniel said. "I'm not trying to prove or justify anything."

#3053. Colts reportedly entertaining Daniel Jones trade discussions ahead of deadline

Multiple teams have inquired about the availability of quarterback Daniel Jones, and the Indianapolis Colts are engaging in those trade talks, ESPN's Adam Schefter and NFL Network's Ian Rapoport reported Saturday.

One team that was reportedly interested was the Baltimore Ravens, who wound up acquiring Jacoby Brissett in a deal with the Arizona Cardinals after both Lamar Jackson and his backup, Tyler "Snoop" Huntley, landed on injured reserve.

The Minnesota Vikings are speculated to be among the other teams considering Jones, according to the ESPN and NFL Network report, as Jones spent part of the 2024 season in Minnesota and has familiarity with a Kevin O'Connell offense that's struggled with Kyler Murray under center.

Jones is set to start for Indianapolis at home against the Dallas Cowboys on Sunday. This past offseason, the Colts inked the 2019 No. 6 overall pick to a two-year, $88 million extension that reportedly included a $44 million signing bonus.

Since, however, Jones — who was coming off a season-ending Achilles tear — hasn't replicated the promising success he achieved during the Colts' 8-2 start to the 2025 campaign, and Indianapolis has moved on from longtime general manager Chris Ballard midseason, embarking on a franchise reset.

On Oct. 27, more than a week following Ballard's firing, the Colts traded Nelson and Buckner. They were two of the organization's longest-tenured players. The six-time All-Pro Nelson went to the Seattle Seahawks in exchange for a 2027 third-round pick and a 2028 fifth-round pick. And the two-time All-Pro Buckner landed back with his old team, the San Francisco 49ers, who gave up a 2027 fourth-round draft pick for the interior defensive lineman.

Indianapolis has dropped four games in a row and is 2-6, sitting in third place in an AFC South the Jacksonville Jaguars are running away with.

The Colts are reportedly open to dealing star running back Jonathan Taylor as well. While Taylor has made it clear he hasn't requested a trade, wide receiver Josh Downs has indicated he wants out of Indy.

With Tuesday's trade deadline fast approaching, eyes are on the Colts, who could make their next move imminently under the direction of interim GM Ed Dodds and the supervision of owner Carlie Irsay-Gordon.

As for Jones, it's possible Indianapolis doesn't move him midseason. The Colts are expected to take calls on him through Tuesday's 4 p.m. ET deadline, according to the ESPN and NFL Network report that surfaced Saturday.

The report also noted Jones has a $10.51 million injury guarantee for the 2027 season that, if he's still on Indianapolis' roster by March 15, will become fully guaranteed. Jones reportedly could waive that injury guarantee to help complete a potential trade.

Or, if the Colts don't trade him, the injury guarantee could be a reason they ultimately bench him, so they don't risk injury and, in turn, that $10.51 million guarantee.

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation and the Federal Reserve Board today published feedback letters for resolution plans submitted in October 2025. The agencies did not identify any shortcomings or deficiencies in these resolution plan submissions.

Resolution plans, also known as living wills, describe a banking organization's strategy for orderly resolution in the event of material financial distress or failure. The agencies conducted a joint review of the 2025 resolution plan submissions from 15 banking organizations with over $250 billion in assets.

In addition, the agencies determined that the shortcoming previously identified in the 2021 BNP Paribas resolution plan has been satisfactorily addressed.

#3050. Investors are doing just as well this year by shunning AI. How to invest if the tech trade keeps fizzling

A version of the S&P 500 without AI briefly pulled ahead of the traditional index this year and is now neck-and-neck as investors rotate into other areas of the market.

A proprietary Goldman Sachs index tracking the S&P 500 without stocks classified as "AI enablers" was up 12.4% through Oct. 22, edging ahead of the standard S&P 500's 12% gain. The Goldman index is now neck-and-neck with the S&P 500 as tech rallied in Monday trading.

Tech and AI-related companies make up about 45% of the S&P 500. That heavy weighting has helped power the index for years, but it's turned into a liability lately; the S&P 500 tech sector is down about 7% this month while other sectors have held up.

Investors have soured on debt and equity issued by companies like CoreWeave and Oracle. Shares of Meta Platforms sank almost 7% Thursday after it outlined more plans to spend heavily on capex and issue additional shares.

Companies supplying power and equipment for the buildout have been hit hard — names like GE Vernova, Bloom Energy, Vistra and Quanta Services all suffered steep declines during the recent selloff.

Goldman, which launched the measure earlier this year with S&P, uses the S&P 500 as its starting universe and excludes companies perceived to be enablers of AI before applying S&P 500 Index weighting methodology to the remaining universe of companies.

The ex-AI index lagged the S&P 500 for most of the year as investors continued to favor chipmakers, cloud-computing companies and other firms benefiting from heavy AI spending. However, that advantage has been narrowing and it disappeared last week.

Meanwhile, investors are rotating into defensive areas. ETFs like State Street Consumer Staples Select Sector SPDR ETF, State Street Health Select SPDR ETF and State Street Utilities Select Sector SPDR ETF are up 7% to 10% this month. Energy is ahead 10% too, though that's more related to the war in Iran.

Some analysts recommend sticking with non-AI companies for diversification and protection against more pain in tech. The "AI pendulum has swung back in favor of non-AI exposure over the last two weeks," Oppenheimer technical analyst Ari Wald said in a note on Oct. 24.

Two industries that Oppenheimer favors now are commercial services and insurance. The firm upgraded those industries to overweight last week, recommending Republic Services, Cintas, Verisk Analytics, Broadridge Financial Solutions, MetLife and Arthur J. Gallagher.

Berkshire Hathaway is also capitalizing on the rotation out of tech. Its Class B shares are up 8% this month, though it's still lagging behind the S&P 500 this year.

Berkshire owns a wide swath of operating companies, including insurance giant Geico and Burlington Northern Santa Fe railroad. It also has a $364 billion stock portfolio including companies like Apple, Coca-Cola, Alphabet and Bank of America. That diversification may help Berkshire continue to outperform if the tech sector drags down the S&P 500.

Trivector Research sees potential in "non-tech compounders." Healthcare giant Eli Lilly is one idea. The stock is up 22% on the strength of Lilly's weight-loss drugs. The company has more in the pipeline, including a next-generation obesity drug that Lilly plans to file for FDA approval early in 2027.

Viking Holdings, a cruise operator, is also proving durable. The company said on its August earnings call that 40% of its river capacity and 60% of its ocean capacity was already booked for 2027. "Based on recent events, we've seen our guests have proved to be resilient and are continuing to book 2027 and future seasons," the company told investors.

Viking stock is up 15% this year but it may still have upside. The average Wall Street target is $109, above recent prices around $82. Barclays recently upgraded the cruise operator to overweight with a $102 target, citing the company's "compelling risk/reward."

#3049. San Antonio vs. Sacramento, Final Score: Spurs’ talent outweighs sloppy moments for 7th straight win, 124-109

The San Antonio Spurs got their first taste of Emirates Cup play on their successful trip to the home of the struggling Sacramento Kings. Despite the Kings being overmatched in talent, they gave a solid effort looking for their second win that made the Spurs sweat at times.

Things started getting ugly as the visitors kept plugging away on the glass and raised their lead to 16 points in the non-Victor Wembanyama minutes thanks in part to their 3-point shooting, ball security and Keldon Johnson's hustle. They went on to have their second-highest scoring first half of the season (76), but had trouble getting back into their flow in the third quarter.

Their large cushion was soon sliced nearly in half until Wemby led the charge that got them back on track. There were some defensive lapses in the following period, but the Spurs quickly atoned for them with a 3-pointer or a move inside.

They were playing like they thought it would be easy early, allowing open 3-point attempts and transition baskets, but the flow of the game started changing when the Spurs started consistently getting to the line. The trickle effect was forcing the Kings to slow down offensively and take more bad shots, which put the Spurs in the transition.

Wembanyama was merciless, scoring 30 points on 55.6 shooting at short and long range, while adding 14 rebounds and two blocks. He took his final rest with eight minutes left as the Spurs were up 22 points.

Darius Acuff Jr. has been the league's highest-scoring rookie, yet he can be a bit of a chucker, so the Spurs defended him mostly without help and in drop coverage on screen rolls. The strategy was more successful in the first half, holding him to 37.5 percent shooting, yet he started getting loose after.

For a while, the Spurs were making mincemeat of the Kings, even having Acuff and Leaky Black frustrated with each other within five minutes of the game. It started with Devin Vassell's early marksmanship helping steady the offense, and then Victor Wembanyama's inside pressure piled on, leaving them without answers. The Kings would also go to a zone when Wemby sat, but the ball movement made them pay for it.

The Kings do a bad job of helping the helper, which resulted in tons of clean outside looks and the Spurs setting a new season high in 3-point percentage. Wemby and Vassell combined for nine 3-point makes.

The Spurs had a rough start to the third quarter, making 25 percent of their attempts through eight minutes. The Kings made four 3-pointers in that span, yet the flow started slowly turning back to the Spurs because the Kings could not defend without fouling, again. To boot, the Spurs went on to shot the most free throws of the season (42).

In the first half, Stephon Castle was too strong on the move to be denied. He wasn't as assertive later but finished an emphatic lob supplied by Wemby. He finished with 19 points on 53.8 percent shooting with seven assists against one turnover.

Crisis averted: Domantas Sabonis committed a flagrant one by sticking his foot under Wembanyama's landing space on a 3-point attempt, causing his ankle to roll. Wemby made two-of-three at the line and subsequently looked fine in the next half.

De'Aaron Fox didn't have the biggest night on the stat sheet, but made good reads that helped take and maintain the lead.

Aside from the trophy, each player on the winning side of the in-season tournament gets $604,985.

#3048. Federal Reserve Board finalizes changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 9:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Wednesday finalized changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements. The two final rules are largely similar to the proposed rules from 2025. The Board also requested comment on a proposal to better capture differences in banks' business models to generate fee income.

The Board conducts stress tests to ensure that large banks are sufficiently capitalized and able to lend to households and businesses even in a severe recession. In December 2024, the Board announced it would modify the test to improve its resilience.

"The stress test is an essential component of our regulatory capital framework," said Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle W. Bowman. "Today's changes preserve its resilience by ensuring that it is transparent, granular, and risk-sensitive. The public will now have greater assurance that the risks banks take will be reflected appropriately in their stress test losses and their capital requirements."

The first final rule requires the Board to invite public input annually on the stress test scenarios and any material model changes. It also updates the framework that guides the design of the hypothetical scenarios and adopts the models that will be used for the 2027 stress test. In addition, the final rule makes several adjustments to the stress test calendar. The final rule also updates the global market shock component, which is a set of shocks to specific market components applied to banks with large trading books. These banks will now be tested against two global market shock components each year, and the Board will use the shock that produces the largest losses for each firm to calculate the firm's stress test results.

The second final rule requires the Board, when calculating stress capital buffer requirements, to average the results from the two most recent annual supervisory stress tests for firms subject to the stress test in both years. The Board will begin averaging stress capital buffer requirements in 2028 to ensure that only models incorporating public input are used in the calculation.

The proposal would request public comment on a revision to the Board's noninterest income model that would improve the stress test's ability to capture business model diversity across firms. If adopted, these adjustments would replace the current noninterest income model that projects each bank's fee income under stress. Comments are due 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

Together, the changes are likely to reduce year-over-year volatility in capital requirements by approximately 50 percent and are not expected to materially affect aggregate capital requirements.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

Federal Register notice: Enhanced Transparency and Public Accountability of the Supervisory Stress Test Models and Scenarios; Modifications to the Capital Planning and Stress Capital Buffer Requirement Rule, Enhanced Prudential Standards Rule, and Regulation LL

Federal Register notice: Request for Comment on Model Changes for the Boardâs 2027 Supervisory Stress Test

#3042. Diving deeper into Cavs blowout win over 76ers: Cleveland plays their most complete game of season

CLEVELAND — The Cleveland Cavaliers avenged their opening-night collapse by thoroughly outplaying the Philadelphia 76ers for 48 minutes, leading to a 119-95 victory.

The Cavs need Mitchell to look to get teammates involved, but that can't come at the expense of his aggressiveness. The start to this game was the prime example of the balance you want to see from Mitchell.

Mitchell got the scoring going with two layups attacking the basket and then a running jumper in transition in the first minute and a half of the game. That opened up avenues for him to find his teammates, which he did during the rest of the first quarter, resulting in three assists.

That opening set the tempo for what was a complete game from Mitchell. He was the undisputed best player on the court. It's no surprise the Cavs won the minutes he played by 28.

"He feels the moment, he knows we needed this win," head coach Kenny Atkinson said. "He made some great passes too. He was very aggressive tonight."

22 points, 6 rebounds, 4 assists, 1 steal, 1 block

Mobley filled in admirably for Jarrett Allen at center. He controlled the paint on both ends and showed his range as a shooter, going 3-for-4 from deep. Comfortably hitting threes like he has been this season opens up Cleveland's entire offense and allows him to easily fit at the four and the five. This was the ideal version of Mobley.

"He's really playing great basketball," Atkinson said.

15 points, 10 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 steal

The Cavs needed rebounding and a scoring punch from the bench considering who was out of the lineup. Hezonja provided both. He took advantage of mismatches inside, going 4-for-4 on shots inside the arc.

Hezonja is slowly becoming an integral part of this team. His presence has become invaluable over the first nine games.

8 points on 3-of-12 shooting, 7 rebounds, 6 turnovers

You could make a convincing argument that the Cavs would be at the top of the conference if James chose them in free agency instead. Cleveland has dropped games due to a failure to close out contests and poor rebounding. James would help immensely in both aspects. That said, those benefits didn't exactly come through in this dreadful performance.

James wasn't able to establish any kind of rhythm. He couldn't create any separation whenever he attacked off the dribble, and he wasn't able to get out into the open court. On top of that, he had only two more points than he had giveaways. That all added up to arguably his worst professional game ever played in Cleveland.

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the comfortable Cavs hoodie HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cavs gear HERE.

Embiid struggled mightily. He had a difficult time getting around Mobley and generating clean looks. This resulted in going 0-for-9 from the field. On the other end, he couldn't move laterally quickly enough to hang with Mobley.

This wasn't a standout offensive performance, but his energy and effort on the glass made him a difference maker. That matters for a player who is being asked to fill very different roles depending on who he's sharing the court with.

Meleek Thomas plays with a confidence that can't be taught. Sequences like this show that.

LeBron got the mismatch against Thomas, except the rookie didn't realize that it was a mismatch. Thomas held his ground, and then forced a steal that led to a basket on the other end.

"He's talented, man," Atkinson said of Thomas.

"Nothing can get as worse as December 2, 2010, from a reception standpoint. I've seen the end of the depths of hell when it comes to reception here, all the way to the heavens above. So anything in between is easy money."

– LeBron James on his return to Cleveland

Cavs fans gave James a respectful, but apathetic welcome home.

The Cavs took 43% of their shots at the rim (92nd percentile). This allowed them to outscore the Sixers 50-30 in the paint.

Cleveland's offense is at its best when it's getting inside. They were able to do that with ease on Friday.

Consistency has been an issue for the Cavs this season. They showed glimpses of how good they can be, but had yet to show it for 48 minutes against a quality opponent. Even in their impressive win over the Minnesota Timberwolves, they struggled for prolonged stretches in the first half. This game, however, was different, due to the contributions of their three stars.

Mitchell, James Harden, and Mobley all fulfilled their roles perfectly. Mitchell was dynamic on ball as a scorer and passer. That energy carried the offense. Harden battled defensively and kept the offense on schedule. His steady hand helped Cleveland maintain their grasp of the game. Mobley controlled the paint on both ends, taking advantage of his slower frontcourt opponents.

There's a downstream effect to the stars playing well. The rest of the rotation was able to more easily slot into their roles, even though they were shorthanded. Players like Meleek Thomas, Tyrese Proctor, and Mario Hezonja have much simpler and more defined roles when they're able to play off the stars and aren't forced to pick up the slack. That makes it easier for them to excel, as they did.

This all added up to a convincing and thorough beatdown of the team with the most wins in the league while also being the antithesis of the opening-night collapse.

The Cavs have shown incredible highs and low lows through nine games. At this point, we don't know who this team will be on a night-to-night basis, and they haven't quite discovered their identity. Yet, the talent is there. If they can put the pieces together, they have the potential to be dangerous.

#3038. Prediction market traders say an Anthropic IPO is near-certain this year after company filed with SEC

Anthropic on Monday unveiled its Form S-1 filing, taking the company one step closer to completing its long-awaited move to the public markets, which is now seen as almost guaranteed to come this year.

Speculators on prediction market platform Kalshi place 95% odds that the artificial intelligence giant officially announces an initial public offering by the end of 2026.

Kalshi defines officially announcing an IPO as either the Securities and Exchange Commission declaring an S-1 effective, a priced IPO or an exchange assigning a ticker to a stock.

The Anthropic S-1 envisions a Nasdaq Stock Market ticker symbol of "ANT."

Anthropic is aiming to start trading the week of Nov. 16, according to a report in the Wall Street Journal. Traders on Kalshi place just a 14% chance that the company begins trading before then.

On the Polymarket platform, traders give a 69% chance that Anthropic will list on a public exchange and open for trading by Nov. 20.

#3036. Colts reportedly ‘not interested’ in moving QB Daniel Jones ahead of trade deadline

According to NFL Insider Tom Pelissero, while they've received inquiries from other teams, the Indianapolis Colts are not considering trading starting quarterback Daniel Jones ahead of next Tuesday's trade deadline:

In particular, the Minnesota Vikings, who have also been connected as a suitor for star Colts running back Jonathan Taylor, have been linked to a potential reunion with Jones ahead of the league's trade deadline.

Incumbent starter Kyler Murray has been battling a 'Grade 2' hamstring strain that will keep him out at least a few weeks and hasn't been all that effective during his debut campaign in Minnesota regardless.

After being previously benched and released by the New York Giants in November of 2024, Jones was initially signed to the Vikings practice squad before being elevated to their active 53-man roster later that same season as one of the team's backups behind entrenched starter Sam Darnold.

McCarthy in the fold in Minnesota, Jones elected to join Shane Steichen in Indianapolis and battle Anthony Richardson Sr. for the Colts QB1 job instead. With Steichen looking increasing unlikely to be retained at year-end, and Jones likely joining him in NFL unemployment as a cap cut this upcoming offseason, it doesn't make sense to me as to why the Colts wouldn't look to clear some of his salary cap off the books, as Jones has a $10.51M injury guarantee for next year (that becomes fully guaranteed on March 15th if he remains on the Colts roster), while also potentially acquiring a 'Day 3' draft pick in the process.

#3035. Luka Dončić drops 53-points amid MVP heroics, 'I'm having a lot of fun'

LOS ANGELES – Walker Kessler had as good a look as anybody inside Crypto.com Arena at the biggest shot of the night.

The Los Angeles Lakers big man grabbed a Sidy Cissoko airball with six seconds left in the first half, handed the ball to Luka Dončić, set a screen for him and let the Luka Magic happen from there. Dončić launched a 62-footer from the top of the opposite key. The crowd held their breath with what felt more like anticipation than anxiety as the ball floated high in the air before coming back down and banking into the net for yet another unreal buzzer-beater.

"He's incredible to watch," Walker said after the Lakers' 124-118 win over the Portland Trail Blazers on Friday. "He's incredible to play alongside. He's my MVP. I think it's pretty clear right now."

Trail Blazers head coach Micah Nori had the utmost praise for Dončić when he spoke with reporters at Crypto.com Arena on Friday afternoon, saying the Slovenian superstar "can do anything in the world."

Dončić proved his opponent's point in the closing seconds of the first half.

What made the shot even more notable, perhaps, was that the Lakers had collectively struggled shooting the ball up to that point, starting 0-for-15 from 3-point range and falling behind by as many as 16. Dončić's shot at the end of the half was just LA's fourth make from deep in 23 attempts; it also sent the Lakers into the locker room having cut the Portland lead to just seven points.

"I think it changes the whole energy, in the team and in the gym. I thought it was an important shot to make," Dončić said before cracking a smile. "So that's why I decided to make it."

But it's not just Dončić who has an unshakeable confidence when it comes to that kind of shot, which he works on meticulously after practices and shootarounds. His teammates are starting to expect them to go in.

"At this point, it's pretty normal for him," Jake LaRavia said. "It's high difficulty for a lot of players, but it's pretty easy for him. I feel like he's just been playing at such a high level. Incredible offensive talent."

And true to what Dončić expected, a switch flipped for the rest of the night.

The Lakers dominated the third quarter, outscoring the Trail Blazers 34-15 in the frame behind 20 points from Dončić, who has now dropped at least 40 in four consecutive games, joining Kobe Bryant, Elgin Baylor and George Mikan as the only players in franchise history to do so. Dončić finished the night with a season-high 53 points, five rebounds and five assists. That brings his average up to an unthinkable 40.1 points per game, something that hasn't been accomplished since Wilt Chamberlain.

"I'm having a lot of fun," Dončić said. "Obviously playing basketball, it's a lot of fun for me. It's more when you play like this and when you win games. It's been like this my whole life. Basketball was my dream. Playing in the NBA was my dream."

The 27-year-old has completely kicked in the door and is dominating the early MVP conversation amid this historic hot streak.

Dončić currently leads Lorenzo Reyes' NBA MVP rankings, and a performance like the one on Friday only furthers the hold Dončić has on the top spot. Just listen to the multiple "M-V-P" chants that echoed through the building at various points in the night.

Not that MVP discussions have much meaning at all 10 games into an 82-game season. Head coach JJ Redick himself said pregame he doesn't get into those talks until they're at least 50 games in. But the level Dončić has been playing at makes it hard not to at least acknowledge it.

Redick, who has previously called Dončić a supernova, was asked if he's running out of superlatives to describe his best player. His response?

"Sure. I'm gonna just sit around and we'll gather one day, come up with more superlatives so I have more words ready," he said sarcastically. "For now, I don't have any words."

The Lakers look like the real deal early on. Their chemistry is off the charts; that much was on full display with their 17 total assists on Friday, or in Kessler's 16-point, 12-rebound night, or Austin Reaves' 25 points.

But Dončić has been the engine behind it all. He's playing like the best player in the NBA, and the team is that much better because of it.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Luka Dončić drops 53-points amid MVP heroics, 'I'm having a lot of fun'

#3032. Luka Doncic scores 53 as Lakers avenge loss to Trail Blazers

The absurd 62-foot three-pointer Luka Doncic hoisted and made late in the second quarter Friday night seemed to ignite him, pushing him to even higher heights on the way to scoring 53 points.

Doncic especially went to work in the third quarter, scoring 20 points in that span in delivering the Lakers to a 124-118 win over the Portland Trail Blazers on Friday night at Crypto.com Arena.

The Lakers had been down by 16 points in the second quarter, but after Doncic's shot, they trailed by only seven and saw the momentum swing.

"Oh yeah, definitely. I think it changes the whole energy in the team and in the gym," Doncic said. "I thought it was an important shot to make. So that's why I decided to make it."

It was the second time Doncic has scored 50 points or more this season, his first time coming when he scored 52 points against the Utah Jazz on Oct. 25. It was the fourth time Doncic has scored 50-plus points as a Laker, passing LeBron James and tying George Mikan for fourth in franchise regular-season history.

It was Doncic's 11th 50-plus point game of his career, which moved him pass Kareem Abdul-Jabbar and tied him with Allen Iverson for the 10th most in NBA history.

Lakers forward Jake LaRavia and Trail Blazers guard Scoot Henderson dive for a loose ball during the first half Friday night at Crypto.com Arena. (Gina Ferazzi / Los Angeles Times)

Doncic played 38 minutes and nine seconds. He shot 17 for 33 from the field and five for 15 from three-point range.

At one point, while Doncic was talking to the media, he blurted out, "I'm tired."

A few minutes later, he was asked how he has been holding up during his offensive onslaught and how far he can keep pushing this.

"Feeling great," Doncic said. "But what do you mean how far?"

"Oh yeah, I feel like that," Doncic said. "But as long as we win, I'm good."

Doncic is now averaging 40.1 points per game this season.

He was asked if he knew the last player to average 40 points per game in a single season.

No, it was not Kobe Bryant, Doncic was told.

It was Wilt Chamberlain, who averaged 50.36 points per game during the 1961-62 season and then 44.83 points per game during the 1962-63 season.

"Man, it's obviously a long season," Doncic said. "We're just 10 games in. We got 72 left. But as long as we win, I'm good. Got them up today, not gonna lie. Hopefully it continues, but you never know."

Doncic added five rebounds and five assists to help the Lakers win their first NBA Cup game after losing the first one to the Golden State Warriors last week.

When the Lakers saw their 16-point lead sliced to four in the fourth quarter, naturally it was Doncic who saved them.

His 7-foot floater with 27.7 seconds remaining gave the Lakers a six-point lead that stood.

Now everyone knows why Doncic is shooting a career-best from three-point range and why he practices those long-range threes all the time.

After Walker Kessler got a defensive rebound with 4.3 seconds left in the second quarter, he passed the ball to Doncic, who had nowhere to go with the Portland defense around him. So, Doncic just tossed up the three-pointer from the three-point line on the Portland side of the court that banked in as time expired. He looked at the crowd as he walked off the court for halftime.

"When he shoots them, it's not a surprise if it goes in," said Austin Reaves, who had 25 points. "So, it's not a high-percentage shot by any means, but he seems to make them a little, you know, easier than the majority of people."

Redick explained why both Bronny James and Cameron Carr were assigned to the Coachella Valley Lakers.

"[The] messaging was very simple," Redick said. "It's just you guys need reps and you guys need to play."

Redick said James and Carr will be with the Lakers on the upcoming trip to Detroit, Atlanta, Milwaukee and Phoenix next week.

Sign up for our weekly newsletter on all things Lakers.

This story originally appeared in Los Angeles Times.

#3024. As AI becomes more fractured, one name stands out: AMD

As Anthropic rushes to takes its shares public, the AI trade is showing cracks, and they are not confined to fringe players.

We recently wrote about CoreWeave, which fell 15.5% on Oct. 14 as yields on its 2032 notes touched 15%, and its default-swap spreads nearly doubled in October. IREN is down more than 20% this year. Last Thursday, the contagion reached chipmakers, which makes sense since much of CRWV and IREN's spending is going there. AMD dropped 8.1% as Meta's financing plans and a supply warning from Lam Research exacerbated data-center fears about the pace of AI spending.

Because when you screen for factors that demonstrably generate outperformance over time, the list narrows quickly. My screen: price above the 200-day moving average; revenue growing at least as fast as the S&P 500 (5%+ per annum); EPS growing at least as fast (7%+); rating upgrades; a PEG below 3; and ROE and ROIC of at least 12%. Of the names reporting in the next two weeks, AMD is effectively the only one that makes the list. Yes, you can (and arguably should, as our staples article described) position your portfolio with slightly less tech exposure than the broad indices provide, but we can't ignore the market's biggest and most transformative sector entirely.

Especially because the growth in this case doesn't just meet my criteria; it blows past them. Consensus for Q3 calls for EPS of $1.94 on revenue of $13.06 billion, versus $1.20 and $9.25 billion a year ago. That's YoY growth of nearly 62% and 41%, respectively. The recent drawdown came from a sector selloff, not anything AMD reported, but it shows that if some names in the space get hit, even the larger players are likely to feel it. On Oct. 6, Lisa Su said AMD would substantially increase supply in 2027 because AI orders keep outrunning capacity.

So how do you balance factors we know work for a fast-growing company against a backdrop of cracks in the AI trade? Options.

Owning the stock outright means absorbing a roughly 9% to 11% implied earnings-related move (day of). Earnings are about a week out, and over the past four quarters the smallest move over a similar window was more than 9%, and the largest move, after the company reported its Q1 26 results, was 38%! Of course, that kind of volatility means options aren't cheap, and that rich premium is also a reason not to simply buy calls.

A call spread risk reversal does both jobs: it reduces risk if the cracks widen, and the short options offset the elevated volatility we would otherwise pay for. The bottom chart shows that, by sacrificing about $1,700 of upside for $5,000 in outperformance during a severe downside move, and given how jittery this trade has been lately, this is a way to stay in it with considerably less risk. $5,000 less, specifically. Of course, selling that out will tie up some cash, but less so than simply buying the stock. And selling both the out and the call reduces long call purchase by 66%

Disclosures: Tidal owns/holds all the securities mentioned in the article.

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

The In-Duress Ratio: He has thrown 5 interceptions to just 2 touchdowns while facing a collapsing pocket.

Under Pressure QBR: His total Quarterback Rating under duress is a microscopic 3.9 out of 100.

This pressure problem directly manifested as the losing variable in all four defeats:

In this deal, Judy Battista has the Colts sending Taylor to the Minnesota Vikings, and in return, Indianapolis receives 2027 second- and third-round picks, along with two 2027 fifth-round selections.

"It's a haul (similar to one that sent RB Christian McCaffrey to the 49ers in 2022), but the Vikings need Taylor's home run threat to pair with QB Kyler Murray's explosive rushing ability," Battista wrote. "The Colts, engaged in a fire sale and potentially hoping to get a first-rounder for Taylor, get plenty of draft picks to help kickstart what looks like a total rebuild."

As a result, could Baltimore be open to trading their veteran players like running back Derrick Henry? In a Nov. 6 article, Wynston Wilcox of FanSided put together a trade idea involving the Vikings and Ravens for Henry. As part of his trade pitch, Wilcox has Minnesota giving up a 2027 third-round pick and a 2027 sixth-round pick (via the New York Jets).

"Minnesota has been rumored to be in play for all the top running back targets at the deadline, including Jonathan Taylor and Tony Pollard," Wilcox wrote. "Why not put Henry on their radar, too?

Derek Carr breaks his silence on reports he is set to join the Minnesota Vikingshttps://sports.yahoo.com/articles/derek-carr-breaks-silence-reports-113000828.html

Vikings News Roundup: Jordan Addison trade idea, Schefter QB watch, Daniel Jones rumorshttps://thevikingage.com/minnesota-vikings-news-jordan-addison-trade-adam-schefter-daniel-jones-rumors

During a recent stream on the YouTube channel of 49ers OnSi reporter Grant Cohn, guest Brandon Deutsch made the suggestion that San Francisco should call the Vikings and offer to trade Jones to Minnesota in exchange for wide receiver Jordan Addison.

"The 49ers [should] call up the Vikings and offer Mac Jones for Jordan Addison.

…[Jones is a] way bigger upgrade than Kyler Murray. Because at least Mac Jones can see the field; he's 6-4. He's not going to run around and not read defenses and twirl the entire game like Kyler Murray.

And he'll get the ball out quick, which I think helps Kevin O'Connell's system a lot because look who benefited in Kevin O'Connell's system: Kirk Cousins. Mac Jones is a very Kirk Cousins-esque quarterback."

'There aren't any weaknesses' | How the Bills offense is preparing to face the blitz-heavy Minnesota Vikingshttps://www.buffalobills.com/news/there-aren-t-any-weaknesses-how-the-bills-offense-is-preparing-to-face-the-blitz-heavy-minnesota-vikings

For the Bills, their focus is on the Vikings' unique defensive scheme, which is blitzing and creating pressure on the quarterback at a historic rate to begin the year.

"They've got so many different looks," QB Josh Allen said. "They play a few different personnel packages where the same looks mean different things. You've got to be in tune with who's on the field and where they're at on the field. So that's up to us and the coaching staff to try to decipher that and figure that out, and just have the best plan that we can have going into it and adjust accordingly."

Through its first seven games of the season, Minnesota has blitzed on 73.6% of dropbacks, according to TruMedia. That blitz rate is far and away leading the NFL, also marking the highest rate for a team in the Next Gen Stats era (since 2016) by a wide margin.

Week 8 featured another level of blitzing for the Vikings, setting the record for the highest blitz rate in a single game (89.7%), after initially taking hold of the record just one week prior (85.2% in Week 7).

"There's a lot more going on that meets the eye from just watching the film," G O'Cyrus Torrence said. "So I feel like it's definitely going to be a lot of bullets flying. A lot of communication is going to have to be going on our part. There's going to be some mess-ups; there's going to be some plays we'll want back, but we have to be able to brush off our minds and get to the next play."

Defensive coordinator Brian Flores has his unit pressuring quarterbacks on 46.4% of dropbacks, leading the NFL.

"It's not even how much they blitz; it's how productive they are when they do blitz," Allen said. "They bring it from both sides, they'll blitz the middle, they'll drop guys that you think are coming. It's a very in-tune defense. Those guys are very smart; they're playing so well together. It's hard to game plan for a team like this because there aren't any weaknesses."

The Vikings have been great at following through on their pressures as well, currently ranked second in the league in both sacks (25) and QB hits (57) through Week 8.

"The easiest way to affect a team is to affect the quarterback in the NFL, and they do it better than anybody, I would say, in the NFL right now," T Spencer Brown said. "It's just about taking pride in what we're doing, making sure we're communicating to the extent we need to."

Flores has found success sending extra rushers in ways offenses haven't been able to figure out, keeping play-callers constantly guessing. Bringing a fleet of Vikings snap after snap has become a mainstay for the highly touted coordinator, giving Bills head coach Joe Brady a lot to gameplan for.

"He's one of the best [defensive coordinators] in the game," Brady said of Flores. "He's evolved; it's not like it's the same defense every year. Continuing to evolve the scheme, the players have a great understanding of how they're attacking protections and coverages. There's a clear identity in the way that they play. If you're a fan of football, you have to enjoy how they play football."

Note: This assumes the team sign Mac Jones and brings back Mason, Hockenson, Brandel, Van Ginkel, Cashman, and Rodgers plus releases Fries and Metellus.

#3020. Victor Wembanyama scores 30 as Spurs beat Kings for 7th straight win

SACRAMENTO, CA ― The San Antonio Spurs continue to roll, earning their seventh consecutive victory in taking down the Sacramento Kings, 124-109, in an NBA Cup group stage game on Friday, Nov. 6.

The Spurs were led by center Victor Wembanyama with 30 points on 10-of-18 shooting. He added 14 rebounds and two blocks. Four other players scored in double figures as Stephon Castle, Devin Vassell and Dylan Harper each had 19. Harrison Barnes scored 13 off the bench.

The Spurs dominated from the opening tip all the way to the end of the game. Wembanyama seems firmly in the early MVP race.

"I do not have a goal for Victor to get MVP," Spurs coach Mitch Johnson said before the game. "I think that there's a lot of things that we're trying to get better and improve on and execute that's gonna be a byproduct of the conversation that could produce."

"He has shown in a short amount of time, in his career, and season by season, however you want, compartmentalized time. His improvement is rapid," he added. "When he gets ahold of certain things, whether that's how he's being guarded, actions we want to run, teammates he's playing with, when he gets ahold of certain things, he can become very dominant. And if he continues to put his energy into the right stuff as he has, and to continue to have that level of retaining what works for him and works for us, then whatever the goals are, I would not bet against."

Spurs guard De'Aaron Fox was welcomed with a mix of boos and cheers as he was announced in the starting lineup. Fox was drafted by Sacramento with the No. 5 pick in the 2017 NBA Draft and spent eight seasons with the Kings before being traded to San Antonio. Fox got the first points on the board on Friday. He finished with nine.

Sacramento only led in the game for a brief moment early in the first quarter. Kings center Domantas Sabonis opened the game with a thunderous slam over Wembanyama. He finished with a double-double, 17 points and 12 rebounds, in the loss.

Kings dynamic scoring rookie Darius Acuff Jr. followed Sabonis' dunk with a 3-point field goal to put Sacramento ahead 5-2 with 10:45 left in the first period. Acuff picked up two early fouls, which sent him to the bench with 6:19 left after playing six minutes in the opening quarter. Acuff finished with 22 points and eight assists. He shot 9-of-20 from the field, including 3-of-9 on 3-point attempts.

The real disparity came with player fouls. The Kings committed 26 fouls as the Spurs shot 42 free throws.

"I mean, early on, it was good, but, I mean, you know, when you get to the free throw line as much as they were, that's tough," Kings head coach Doug Christie said. "Because not only are they a good defensive team, they're even better when their defense is set, and that's what the free throw allows you to do. When we were able to get in open court, we were able to find some things and create the type of 3-point shots and different things, collapse in the defense."

The Kings have back-to-back losses after getting their first win of the season against the Dallas Mavericks in a 115-100 finish on Nov. 2. Sacramento welcomes the Miami Heat on Sunday, Nov. 9 to the Golden 1 Center.

The Spurs won the first game of their four-game road trip. They immediately board a plane to face the Portland Trail Blazers on Saturday, Nov. 7.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Victor Wembanyama scores 30 as Spurs beat Kings for 7th straight win

#3013. Why Joe Mazzulla hugged Neemias Queta instead of yelling at him in win over Hornets

CHARLOTTE — Early in the third quarter of the Celtics' 119-80 win over the Charlotte Hornets, Joe Mazzulla called a timeout and walked over to Neemias Queta.

Queta had just gotten tangled up with multiple Hornets players while trying to secure a rebound. And, as is often the case, Mazzulla grew frustrated with his big man's longstanding tendency to fall.

But this time, instead of reaming him out, Mazzulla chose to embrace him.

"I'm just trying to channel my anger into love," Mazzulla said afterward.

"Instead of just yelling at him, I gave him a hug."

It's no secret that Mazzulla is hard on the Portuguese big man, who began his journey with the Celtics on a two-way contract in 2023 and blossomed into the Celtics' productive and reliable starting center last season.

Sometimes, Mazzulla's yelling gets picked up by the TV broadcast. More often than not, his anger is visible from press row.

And, none of that tough love is something that the Celtics head coach denies.

"I'm hard on Neemi," Mazzulla said on Friday night. "I'm hard on our bigs. Those guys, the way they need to play for us, there's a ton of pressure on them. We talk about it all the time — the fives have the toughest job on this team because they got to do a ton on both ends of the floor."

Mazzulla has frequently discussed how difficult the center position is in the team's system, particularly as of late. That's also applied to Mitchell Robinson, who has struggled in his acclimation to the Celtics as he takes on more responsibility in a new system.

Still, Mazzulla wants to make sure that amid his tough coaches style, there's an understanding that all of it is rooted in love. He visits players in the offseason in order to better understand what makes them tick; this past summer, he joined Queta in Portugal, Mitchell Robinson in Nashville, and Luka Garza in Iowa.

In difficult moments, there's a relationship that runs deeper than basketball.

"I appreciate that they allow me to be hard on them, but I also want them to know I appreciate them," he said. "Just trying to do that for them."

Queta is not the only player to get a hug from Joe Mazzulla as of late; Payton Pritchard got an in-game hug from Mazzulla in a win over the Chicago Bulls, after a couple of high-impact plays.

"Joe challenged me," Pritchard said after that win. "I feel like the first four games my defensive activity and my rebounding have been poor… He's challenging me to step up in that area. I think the hug was for me doing that."

On Friday, Pritchard reflected on how Mazzulla has successfully tapped into both sides of the emotional spectrum as a coach.

"Joe, he's a competitor, and he has a lot of emotion, so it brings the best out of us, and it's also cool when you are doing well; he shows the emotion of a loving brother type situation," Pritchard said. "And then we'll have moments where we get into it, and we have disagreements, and it's all right. It's just part of it — we go through love and hate."

Pritchard said his bond with Mazzulla has strengthened over the years; he's been playing for him since he took over as head coach in 2022, and it's come with plenty of variance. Pritchard has gone through stretches in which he's fallen out of the rotation, won Sixth Man of the Year, and was a part of the 2024 championship team.

"It's a journey," he said. "We've definitely gone through a lot of different situations, a lot of ups and downs, and you just grow your relationship through that, and it becomes stronger and stronger."

Mazuzlla echoed that sentiment last week, after his hug.

"I like pushing him, I like coaching him, and I love him like a brother," Mazzulla said of Pritchard.

For Mazzulla, turning anger into love has been the result of an intentional effort, especially when it comes to his relationship with Queta.

"A lot of meditation, a lot of journaling, a lot of just not being so mad at him all the time," Mazzulla said. "But I love him."

Still, Mazzulla's ability to leverage anger hasn't gone away, the Celtics' veterans say.

"It just depends on the day," said Jayson Tatum with a smile. "Some good days, some bad days."

"They're both needed," Pritchard said. "You can't have love without the anger."

#3011. 5 Vikings to zero-in on vs. Bills

The Buffalo Bills and the Minnesota Vikings are set to face off on Monday night in a battle of two 6-1 teams. The Bills are the current No. 1 seed in the AFC, and while the Vikings are the No. 2 seed, depending on the result of the San Francisco 49ers' matchup with the Las Vegas Raiders on Sunday, Minnesota could be playing as the technical No. 1 seed when their game kicks off Monday night.

The Bills currently have the NFL's top scoring offense, as head coach Joe Brady's bunch averages 33.3 points per game so far this season. Minnesota, on the other hand, has the NFL's top scoring defense, as defensive coordinator Brian Flores' charges allow just 13.3 points per game so far this year. Minnesota's offense is ranked just 27th in the league; Buffalo's defense is ranked just 24th.

In a lot of ways, these two teams are polar opposites; however, they're similar in the most important way: They win football games. And, if Buffalo wants to improve their mark to 7-1 this season, they'll need to turn in another stellar defensive performance against a backup quarterback, albeit one with much more starting experience than their last opponent.

Here are five Vikings to watch on Monday night._____________________________________________________________________________

In some ways, having Wentz start the game might present greater problems for Buffalo than Kyler Murray, who is Minnesota's normal starter. Murray is out with a sore hamstring, and while he is a great scrambler, he's not as good a pure passer as Wentz is. Wentz was once rather mobile, but injuries and age have slowed him down, and he's not close to the player he was when he was an MVP front-runner with the Philadelphia Eagles back in 2019.

I expect the Bills to take a page out of the Brian Flores playbook—or the Week Eight Jim Leonhard playbook, even—by blitzing Wentz early and often. Buffalo blitzed on nearly two-thirds of their defensive snaps last week. Doing that again while setting a solid edge will keep Wentz in the pocket and cause him to process quickly. If he has time to throw, he'll find receivers. If he's rushed, he'll look to dump the ball off before taking big hits, which could lead to some turnovers for a Buffalo defense looking for a second straight week of solid play.

Jefferson will always be a "what-if" for Buffalo, even if that characterization isn't necessarily fair. Yes, he was drafted by the Vikings with the first-round pick Buffalo sent to Minnesota in exchange for Stefon Diggs. While some people say that the Bills just could have drafted Jefferson rather than making the trade, there was no way to tell exactly what Jefferson would become, or even if he would be available when Buffalo picked. If draft choices are lottery tickets, stud veterans are closer to sure bets, and for the better part of four seasons, Diggs was as sure a bet as there was in the NFL.

With all that said, Jefferson is Buffalo's problem this week, and I'd expect to see No. 47, Christian Benford, lined up across from him more often than not. If Jefferson is matched up against literally anyone else in Buffalo's secondary, I'd expect Wentz to throw that way. If he's covered by Benford, we at least know that it's Buffalo's best on Minnesota's best. That matchup is one of the biggest keys of the game.

The veteran is a Brian Flores staple, as he was teamed with Flores during his time with the Miami Dolphins. As a result, the Bills and quarterback Josh Allen are quite familiar with Van Ginkel and his flowing locks. The big edge rusher is having a great year so far, as he leads the Vikings with 6.5 sacks and seven tackles for loss.

Guard Alec Anderson did not practice on Thursday with an ankle issue, and tackles Dion Dawkins (rib) and Spencer Brown (back) were out of practice and limited in it, respectively. Buffalo's offensive line is beat up a little bit, and it's not a great time for that to happen. No time is great for a banged-up offensive line, but a date with the league's No. 1 defense is basically the worst time for that to be the case.

Regardless of who's at tackle, the Bills will have to account for Van Ginkel on Monday.

They'll also have to look out for Van Ginkel's teammate, as Turner has six sacks and a team-high 19 quarterback hits this season. The third-year man is improving on his breakout 2025 campaign, and he's on pace to surpass his 2025 sack total (8) by plenty this season. At the risk of sounding like I just teleported in from 1992, Buffalo's best bet to slow the pass rush might be to establish the running game early.

Play-action passes on first down can give the Vikings' edge rushers pause, and some solid second-down runs can slow them further. Minnesota is allowing opponents to average 4.5 yards per rush, and given that the Bills average 4.8 yards per rush on the season, it's a strength on offense and a weakness for Minneosta's defense in what has otherwise been an outstanding unit.

This might be a James Cook III day moreso than a Josh Allen day if Buffalo is going to come out on top. Cook can help keep his quarterback clean by breaking some big runs, which should hopefully make the Vikings' stellar edge rushers hesitate before pinning their ears back and going full-force into pass-rush mode.

No name bias here, but Murphy is a stud. He's allowing just a 57.3 quarterback rating this season when he's the closest man in coverage, as he has three interceptions and six pass breakups while allowing zero touchdowns on 40 pass attempts his way. He's really playing better than he has at any point in his career. Buffalo's passing offense has been outstanding, but against the league's No. 1 pass defense, it might be wise to avoid the guy who's currently having a career-year. The problem, of course, is that James Pierre and Isaiah Rodgers are also playing extremely well.

What if I told you that, despite the numbers listed above, Murphy is still the guy allowing the greatest percentage of completed passes on this defense, as he's allowed a 65% completion rate for 310 yards. Compared to Pierre (47.6%, 20.2 passer rating against) and Rodgers (33.3% competion rate, 34.7 passer rating against), Murphy might actually be the one performing the worst at the moment.

This is a really good defense. The Bills will have to pick their poison. Murphy might be it.

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. #3008. Ripple strikes deal with Robinhood to expand its RLUSD digital dollar

Ripple has struck a deal with Robinhood to expand the reach of its digital dollar, RLUSD, strengthening trading and financing links between their institutional businesses and improving Robinhood's internal money management.

The partnership involves using RLUSD to settle institutional trades, automate Robinhood's treasury operations and expand access to digital asset trading and financing through Ripple Prime and Bitstamp, the global crypto exchange acquired by Robinhood last year.

It also helps meet Robinhood's ambition of becoming a global financial superapp, centralizing its customers' financial lives while expanding the underlying infrastructure that can serve both retail and institutional clients.

"Utility is what moves stablecoins forward. Working with Ripple, Bitstamp and Robinhood will have access to RLUSD for institutional settlement, Ripple Treasury for our operations and Ripple Prime for financing," Robinhood Crypto General Manager Johann Kerbrat said in a statement shared with CNBC. "That is what a connected, global financial system looks like."

Stablecoins have long been a workhorse of the crypto world, giving traders a way to move money among exchanges, buy and sell crypto assets, and park funds between trades without having to convert back into traditional currency. But as the crypto industry evolves beyond trading and markets move toward 24/7 liquidity, stablecoins are becoming essential financial infrastructure because of their ability to enable near-instant settlement and move dollar-denominated value at any time, regardless of traditional banking hours.

As firms look to do more with stablecoins beyond simply listing them on exchanges, stablecoin issuers are betting they'll favor a one-stop-shop approach that bundles the technology and services needed to move money, settle trades and manage cash.

"Stablecoins have evolved from a crypto trading tool into a critical piece of financial infrastructure," Ripple President Monica Long told CNBC. "The clearest evidence of this is where they're now being embedded — from corporate payments to capital markets. Our partnership with Robinhood reflects that evolution."

"This is about much more than trading," she added. "It's about fundamentally changing how money moves and is managed — giving institutions the ability to operate 24/7/365, unlocking liquidity and putting capital to work more efficiently."

Circle Internet Group, a Ripple competitor, is positioning itself for a similar future, where winning platforms, in addition to issuing stablecoins, also provide the infrastructure to put them to work. Earlier this year, for example, it launched the Circle Payments Network, connecting financial institutions and payment providers using its flagship USDC stablecoin and other digital assets for cross-border payments.

The network is Circle's answer to the 8-year-old Ripple Payments business, a service that helps businesses move money across borders, using any of several assets moving through that system, including USDC, Ripple-created XRP and, as of its debut in December 2024, RLUSD.

RLUSD's market cap has grown to more than $2.5 billion. Tether's USDT and Circle's USDC still dominate the total stablecoin market, which now exceeds $300 billion.

RLUSD will also be listed on Robinhood's trading platform, and is currently available to traders on Bitstamp as well as Robinhood Chain, the company's home-grown blockchain for tokenized stocks and other assets.

Robinhood, which reports third-quarter results for the three months ended in September after the market closes, rose about 2.5% in early trading Tuesday, snapping a three-day decline.

Correction: A previous headline misspelled RLUSD.

#3002. Most of Bills Mafia now confident in Buffalo ahead of Week 9 tilt w/Vikings

After watching the Buffalo Bills thrash the mostly listless Baltimore Ravens in Week 8, Bills Mafia is once again buying what Buffalo's selling. Now 6-1 and in sole possession of first place in the AFC, the Bills currently control their playoff destiny. Hopefully that includes earning the one seed, but there's plenty of football to play before deciding that situation.

As is true with most seasons since quarterback Josh Allen rose to NFL royalty, fans have the biggest expectations of Buffalo — things like AFC Championships and Super Bowl wins. Everyone understands it's also true that time as constructed is limited, and that Allen loses opportunity with each passing season.

Reading the tea leaves, that idea loomed large in co-owner Terry Pegula's decision to fire former head coach Sean McDermott. Time is short, and much of it had been "wasted" heading into this season. Accolades, stats, and places in the sport's history are each incredible to claim in their own right, yet they pale when considering that elusive Lombardi Trophy.

Enter new head coach Joe Brady and his equally new defensive coordinator Jim Leonhard. Both men get their first shot at NFL stardom with arguably the NFL's biggest and most-talented star leading the way. No pressure to be perfect, right?

That's just it. Perfection is all but guaranteed, yet that's what so many Bills fans have demanded of the 2026 season because it seems absurd that Allen hasn't so much as even played in a Super Bowl to this point in time. Nevermind that, yes, the team does have a first-time head coach and defensive coordinator. Nevermind that the defense underwent a huge overhaul in scheme. Nevermind that other NFL teams have the same goals.

Somehow, beating a downtrodden Ravens team that filtered through to its emergency quarterback was all that some still-skeptical fans needed to express their support of the Bills this season. Our latest Reacts results reveal that 86% of voters are confident in the team's direction under Brady and president of football operations/general manger Brandon Beane. It's a jump of 23 percentage points from last week's 63% confidence rating.

The defense played a fantastic game, and quite honestly safety Damar Hamlin looked like the perfect fit as an emotional and schematic leader for Leonhard's system. If nothing else, it speaks to the potential of an aggressive and tough defense lying in wait. That complementing the league's top-scoring offense (and one in first by a large margin) should be enough to get Buffalo where is needs to be by season's end. But they must continue playing this brand of football, and find the right amount of luck at the right time.

The Bills get to play another purple-clad team in Week 9, this time hoping to put a stamp on their signature offensive attack in front of another national audience. If you're still unsure about Buffalo this season, what has to happen to change your mind?

We also asked Rumblers to weigh in with their positional wishlist ahead of the NFL trade deadline. Despite the great game Hamlin played, it's likely unsurprising that the safety position led our poll results. We're not talking by just a few percentage points, either.

A massive 72% of votes went toward adding a safety. The next closest position was linebacker. Wide receiver garnered just 9% of the vote, and only 3% of voters saw fit to add elsewhere to the Bills' roster.

As if you needed a reminder, the results from one of this week's national polls revealed that Josh Allen is viewed as the NFL MVP through Week 8.

Buffalo's opponent in Week 9, the Minnesota Vikings, also sport a 6-1 record. The Vikings have a championship-caliber defense that might not be getting enough attention at the moment. But the offense has been a bit inconsistent too often with either Kyler Murray or Carson Wentz at quarterback.

Now, rumors suggest Minnesota could be in the market to trade for a quarterback. A lot of that will depend on how things go during Monday Night Football. However, it appears the situation on offense has the SKOL family second-guessing the team's direction this season.

Mind you, Minnesota just lost and it's their first defeat of the season. That somehow has just 17% of Vikings fans confident in the current direction of their favorite team.
